572. MARY McCARTHY was indicted for stealing on the 4th of February, 1 pillow-case, value 6d., 2 napkins, value 1s.; 1 waistcoat, value 6d., and 1 pair of stockings, value 2s., the goods of Nathan Mandleson.
PHOEBE MANDLESON. I am the wife of Nathan Mandleson, who lives in Petticoat-lane, and is a pastry-cook. The prisoner had been in our service a week and three days - I had given her warning, and she was about to leave us. On the 4th of February she came to the counter, out of doors, for me to pay her her wages - I thought she appeared much stouter than usual; I told her to go into the kitchen - I went, and asked her what she had in her pocket; she said a couple of dirty caps - I desired to look at them, but instead of dirty caps it turned out to be a bundle of coffee tied up in a piece of rag which she had found about the house - I then made her pull off her pockets, and found in them some more coffee, fancy biscuits, sugar and bread - I told her I would search her, and she ran up stairs into the back bed-room - I called my husband to mind the place, and in a few minutes I followed her up stairs - she was then in the act of throwing some dirty clothes from her, but I cannot say what they were, as there were a great many dirty things about; I found upon her a pillow-case, two napkins, and a flannel - I sent for an officer, and gave her in charge; these are the articles I found on her - this pair of stockings was pulled off her in the room when she was committed: they are all my husband's property - there were other articles on the floor in the room where she was undressing, but I cannot say that she pulled them all off.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. Was not the room quite covered with dirty things? A. No, not quite- it was not our washing-day; we had washed three days before; there were a great many rough dry things about, which had been dried the day before - she had the flannel waistcoat on, with her arms through the sleeves, and the stockings were on her feet; she had a small bundle, but none of my property was there - I found some of her things in the house afterwards, which I gave to her cousin; there was a pair of stockings, and a bundle of things - I saw the officer with her bundle in his hand, but I did not exactly look at it - I was too much confused; I cannot say it was not as large as this bundle.
JAMES SMART. I took the prisoner, but did not see any thing produced from her person.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not she say she had put them on by mistake? A. Yes, and that she was going out washing.
Prisoner's Defence. It was my own stockings she brought against me; her stockings were not worth 3d.; I had been out, and got mine wet in the snow - I put that old pair on, left mine there, and when she came against me, she said she could not bring any thing against me but my own stockings - I was not going to leave her; I got up at three o'clock in the morning to wash, and I put that old jacket on to keep me warm - the napkin I had no business with; the pillow-case and napkin were in the room, and she said I had them on me, which I had not - the coffee and other things were under the bed.
PHOEBE MANDLESON. I carried the coffee and sugar up in my hand which I had taken from her in the kitchen -I spilt some on the bed; she had got biscuits and fancy bread; I had had no quarrel with her; I was going to send her to another place - she did not suit me; it had not snowed that morning; she had been out some days before- she had not been washing that day; I am positive I took the pillow-case from her - I unpinned it from round her person.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor and Jury.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18310217-146]
573. JOHN MURPHY was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 2 planes, value 7s.; 2 saws, value 12s.; 9 chisels, value 3s.; 2 screw-drivers, value 2s.; 3 guages, value 1s.; 1 square, value 2s.; 1 hammer, value 3s.; 1 pair of pincers, value 2s.; 1 spoke-shave, value 9s.; 1 divider, value 2s.; 2 gimblets, value 3d.; 3 punches, value 3d.; 1 scraper, value 4d.; 3 brad-awls, value 3d.; 1 brush, value 3d., and 1 basket, value 1s., the goods of John Whitaker.
JOHN WHITAKER. I live in Stafford-place, Pimlico.On the 17th of August I was at work at the Board of Work's office, Whitehall - I left my basket of tools within the hall door; I missed them at half-past eleven o'clock that morning.
THOMAS CRANE. I lodge at the Black Bull public-house, Chapel-street, Westminster. On the morning of the 17th of January I was at Whitehall; the prisoner passed me with a basket of carpenter's tools - he crossed the street right opposite the Horse Guards; a person I met said something to me - I went and looked behind a place at the Horse Guards, where I found the basket and the articles stated; it appeared to me to be the same as I saw the prisoner carrying; I pointed him out to the officer, who took him.
WILLIAM MOORE. I am a private in the Life Guards. On the 17th of January I was sentinel, and saw the prisoner between eleven and twelve o'clock place a basket behind the door which leads to the stable; I told Mr. Crane where it was - I am certain the prisoner placed it there.
JOHN WHITAKER. This is my basket and my tools - I saw the prisoner pass two or three times while I was at work that morning, and I missed them about three minutes after I had seen him the last time.
NATHANIEL NICHOLLS. I am a Police-constable. I took the basket from behind the stable-door, and I took the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-147]
574. JAMES NEIL and JOSEPH PARROCK were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of January, 1 cwt. of potatoes, value 3s. 6d., the goods of John Daly.
JOHN DALY. I live at No. 13, Saffron-hill, in the back and front lower rooms. On the 12th of January I had 1 cwt. of potatoes there - a person must go through the passage from the street to get to the room they were in; I went to bed between eleven and twelve o'clock on the 12th of January, in the front room, and the next morning, when I awoke I missed my potatoes from the back room - the staple of that door had been drawn; I applied to an officer, and Neil, who lodged in the same house, was taken - Parrock and Neil's sister were in the room with him; there were about - cwt. of potatoes in their room - some were in a sack, and some in a pail; Neil said it was done through distress, for he had had nothing to eat for three days - Parrock said they were Neil's mother's; I lost some washed potatoes, but I did not find any of them - those I found were red Scotch potatoes; I do not know whether they were what I lost - they were worth 3s. 6d.; I had locked the back room at eight o'clock, and did not go to it again before I went to bed; Neil's sister had lodged there, but she left the day that he was taken.
WILLIAM MASON. I am a Police-constable, and live in Greville-street. I went into the room with the prosecutor, and found the two prisoners - Neil said he took the potatoes through distress, and he hoped he would forgive him - Parrock said the same; they appeared to be in great poverty.
The prisoners handed in a petition for mercy, pleading extreme poverty. They received a good character.
NEIL - GUILTY. Aged 17.
PARROCK - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18310217-148]
575. BENJAMIN SPICER was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of February, 1 tea-pot, value 2s., the goods of Robert Winfield.
ELEANOR LARNER. I am servant to Mr. Robert Winfield, who keeps the Bricklayers' Arms, in Whitecross-street . On the Tuesday before the 4th of February I was in the parlour of his house - there is a window, through which I could see a bottle-rack; I saw the prisoner come out of the public-room, and down some stairs, take the tea-pot off the bottle-rack, empty the water out of it, and give it to his wife, who put it into her apron; she went through the yard, then through a shop, and out into the street- the prisoner went up stairs again; I saw the Policeman at the door, and he brought back the woman, with the tea-pot - this is it; I know it by having burnt the lip of it off.
GEORGE GLADWELL. I am a Police-constable. I stopped the prisoner's wife with the tea-pot - I then went back, and took the prisoner.
ELIZABETH WINFIELD. I am the wife of Robert Winfield. This is my tea-pot - I have had no quarrel with the prisoner.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that a man named Pascall, who he saw at a public-house, had sent him to fetch the tea-pot to dispose of for him. He received a good character.
ELIZABETH WINFIELD. I know nothing of a person named Pascall - I do not know whether he was in the house; I believe the prisoner did say a man named Pascall had sent him for it.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-149]
576. EDWARD STRANGE was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of February, 3 shovels, value 6s., the goods of James Hayward.
JAMES HAYWARD. I am an ironmonger, and live in Aylesbury-street, Clerkenwell. A little after five o'clock on the 9th of February, in consequence of some information, I went out of my house, and met John Pett - I went, by his direction, to some ruins on the Woodbridge Estate, where I found three shovels of mine; I took them to my house.
JOHN PETT. I live in Garrett's-buildings, and am a carpenter. I was in Aylesbury-street on the evening of the 9th of February - I met the prisoner and another very near the prosecutor's shop; the prisoner had three shovels - I went in the direction of the Woodbridge Estate, and saw him throw the shovels down; he was taken in my presence - he was not more than twelve yards from the prosecutor's shop.
EVAN DAVIES. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner on the night of the 9th of February - I took him into custody- I went back to the prosecutor's shop, and took these shovels.
JAMES HAYWARD. These are my shovels - they had been in the street; they are generally chained, but were not then.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the witness was mistaken in his person. He received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 1 Month.
[trialAccount t18310217-150]
Fourth Middlesex Jury before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
577. JOHN WISE was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of January, 1 coal-sack, value 7s., the goods of Henry Papps and another, his masters.
HENRY TRAWARTHERS. I met the prisoner in the Strand, at one o'clock in the morning of the 31st of January - he had this coal-sack under his frock; knowing him, I followed him, and took him to the station-house - the sack had nothing in it; I suppose he was half a mile from the Adelphi-wharf.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Where was this? A. Near the corner of Adam-street; he appeared as if he had been drinking, but on the way to the station-house he tried to run away, and I was forced to knock him down to take him again.
THOMAS BOTTOMLEY. I am in partnership with Mr. Henry Papps - we are coal-merchant s, at the Adelphi-wharf. The prisoner was in our service five months - this sack is ours; he had no right to have it - it did not belong to the waggon he went with.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he in your service at this time? A. Yes - we very seldom send out single sacks of coals, when we do it is in the day time; if a man had got tipsy, it would not have been an offence for him to have brought the sack back on Monday.
COURT. Q. When you send out a sack you send it with coals in it? A. Yes, and they go in two sacks in general.
WILLIAM HAMBLY. I am a carman to the prosecutors. I know this is one of their sacks.
Cross-examined. Q. Has it any mark on it? A. No, but I have used it, and know it was one of the set of sacks which was brought out on the Monday, and this was on the Saturday night - this has W. H. on it.
Prisoner's Defence. I was employed to trounce the waggon - the sack was used to carry some other sacks in, and I supposed it was left at the Cock and Bottle; I took it to take it home the next morning - I could not get any money of Hambly, and Mr. Bottomley would not pay me.
JURY to MR. BOTTOMLEY. Q. In whose employ was the prisoner? A. In mine - I pay Hambly, and he pays him; I direct the carmen in the trouncers they employ -- they take them from the wharf, but they are approved of by me; there had been no coals sent to the Cock and Bottle - one or two persons on the wharf give their men the sweepings, but I do not; I give them 1l. 2s. 6d. a week instead of the sweepings.
COURT to HENRY TRAWARTHEN. Q. Where did you meet the prisoner? A. Near the bottom of the Shades, walking from the Adelphi towards Temple-bar.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he going from the Cock and Bottle? A. Yes, certainly.
GUILTY. Aged 36. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-151]
578. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 2 salt-cellars, value 1s. 4d., the goods of Henry Chandler.
JOHN HINCKLIFF. I saw the prisoner on the 12th of February, take a pair of salts off Mr. Chandler's board, just by Mile-end turnpike - he put them into his pocket; he did not come into the house at all - there were one or two more with him; I called my master, Mr. Chandler, and we caught the prisoner in Bellevoe-place, Red Cow-lane - the salts were found upon him.
HENRY CHANDLER. I heard the alarm - I caught the prisoner with these salts, which are mine; they had been opposite my window.
The prisoner delivered in a petition for a lenient sentence. He received a good character, and a witness engaged to employ him.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310217-152]
579. ANN McDONALD, alias JOHANNA BUCKLEY , was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of January, 1 cap, value 2s., and 3 brushes, value 3s., the goods of David Cohen.
DAVID COHEN. I live in Cannon-street East. The prisoner was in the habit of coming to work as an apprentice to a woman in my house - on the 21st of January I had nobody there but her and the woman; on the following morning I went into the wash-house to clean my shoes, and I missed the brushes - no one but her could have taken them; I have seen them since - I had not missed the cap till I found it.
WILLIAM McDONALD. The prisoner brought these things to my house at Bermondsey - her name is not McDonald, but Buckeridge; she told me she bought the cap, and the brushes were her own.
WILLIAM DICKINSON. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner, and found this property at McDonald's.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. These are my own brushes, and I bought the cap.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310217-153]
580. HENRY SAUNDERS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of January, 2 drinking-glasses, value 2s., the goods of Moses Crafter.
ANNA MARIA CRAFTER. I am the wife of Moses Crafter - he keeps a public-house in Ratcliff-highway. On the 22nd of January the prisoner came with another person for a shilling for two sixpences; while they were there I missed a glass - they had been there the day before for change, and then I missed a glass: I took them on the 22nd, and found this glass under the prisoner's coat - both the glasses had been taken from the counter; I called the Police-constable, and the prisoner was taken - the other boy ran away.
THOMAS GAMMAGE. I took the prisoner - I found one sixpence, and one shilling on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I never had the glass.
GUILTY. Aged 14 - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310217-154]
581. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of February, 11 pieces of foreign silver coin, called dollars, value 2l. 4s., the monies of Edward Norton.
EDWARD NORTON. I am a seaman, employed on board the London packet. I had a trunk containing some property, and about twenty dollars, as near as I can guess- I left it in the chamber adjoining where I slept, at Elizabeth Young 's; the prisoner at first lodged in the same room; he was sent to that lodging by the American Consul - he was there three or four weeks; I lost the key of my trunk, and got a new one - on the 5th of February I missed from eight to twelve dollars from my trunk; I know one of them was particularly marked with blackspots on the face - I had an old key filed to fit my lock; I do not know what became of the first key - I had it in my pocket one afternoon, and the next morning it was gone - the prisoner then slept in the same room.
ELIZABETH DAVIES. I have known the prisoner about five weeks; he has been in the habit of coming backwards and forwards to me - we had lived together; he gave me eight dollars to get changed, and two he had given me before; I changed them at Mr. Cushway's, at the Royal Sovereign, and took two and 2s. to the Angel, to get half a sovereign for them, and two I took to a pawnbroker's in Ratcliff-highway, but I do not know the name - the prisoner told me he wanted the half-sovereign; I got it, and gave it him on last Friday fortnight.
WILLIAM CUSHWAY. I keep the Royal Sovereign. I received five dollars from Davies, I think, on the 5th of February - I gave her 4s. for each dollar; I gave them to Mitchell.
WILLIAM MITCHELL. I received these five dollars from Cushway: I had taken the prisoner on the day before; I asked if he had any money about him; he said none whatever - I asked if he had any dollars; I searched and found on him half a sovereign and a duplicate for a pair of trousers.
MARY WATERS. My husband keeps the Angel public-house, Back-road, Shadwell. I received two dollars and 2s. from Davies for a half-sovereign - we take a good many; we value them at 4s. each; we had twenty-two dollars in the till - I cannot say how many I received that day; we have a drawer in which we put all the foreign silver - we have a good deal of foreign silver.
THOMAS WOOD. I am waiter at the Ship and Shears. The prisoner gave me a dollar to change one Friday morning, more than a fortnight ago; I took it to a pawnbroker, and got him 4s. for it.
EDWARD NORTON. Here is one dollar I can swear to by the black marks on it.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-155]
582. CHARLES STRIKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 1 coat, value 1l., the goods of Thomas Wilson.
THOMAS WILSON. I live in Hoxton Old-town. On the 12th of January, I had just driven my gig up to a stable, facing Shoreditch church, about half-past eleven o'clock at night; I got out of the gig, had just rung the bell, and on turning round I saw the prisoner take the coat from the side of my gig - he pulled it off, hung it on his arm, and was walking off with it, away from me; I went up to him, and said, "What do you do with that coat, you scoundrel?" the Policeman came, and took him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What are you? A. A publican; I keep the Plough - I did not perceive that the prisoner was tipsy; he said it was a lark, or something of that kind - he had got about two yards from the gig.
JOHN MASTERS. I was with the prosecutor - I got out of the gig first; I saw the prisoner take the coat - he was walking away with it; he did not appear to be tipsy.
THOMAS HURRAN. I am the officer. I took the prisoner and the coat.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that he was intoxicated; and, in the act of falling down, had caught hold of the coat to save himself, when he was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-156]
583. NATHAN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of January, 1 half-sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 1 pair of gloves, value 1s. 6d., and 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the property of John Hopkins, his master.
JOHN HOPKINS. I live in Shoreditch, and am a linen-draper; the prisoner was my porter - he slept in the shop with another person. On the night of the 21st of January we found we had been robbed of 16s.; and on the Saturday night following I marked a quantity of money, and left it in the desk - on the Sunday morning I went to the desk, and missed one half-sovereign, two half-crowns, and four shillings in silver; I had marked the silver, but not the half-sovereign - I sent for an officer, and had the two porters examined, up stairs; he found on the prisoner this half-sovereign, and the two marked half-crowns.
THOMAS GARTON. I am an officer. I found on the prisoner the half-sovereign and two half-crowns, which the prosecutor spoke to, and 1l. 2s. 6d. besides.
JAMES SIMMONDS. I produce these gloves and handkerchief, and the key of the box, which were found in the prisoner's trunks.
MR. HOPKINS. These gloves and handkerchief were taken from the prisoner's trunk, up stairs, in the dressing-room; the prisoner said they were mine, but I have no mark on them - these half-crowns have my mark on them, and the half-sovereign had a little mark at the end of the A, which I saw - I did not put any mark on it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did not the prisoner say, "This handkerchief is mine?" A. No. he said, "It is yours, Sir;" I had not left any sovereign in the desk - the cashier, generally, takes out all the money at eight o'clock; he did so that night, but I was not there - I put the money into the desk, between eleven and twelve; the prisoner might have access to the desk, but he had no business there - he did not say, in my presence, that he had put a sovereign in, and taken the change out - I had a very good character with him.
JURY. Q. Was it possible for him to have put a sovereign into the desk? A. I counted all that the desk contained when I marked the money; the sum total was about 2l. 15s., and from that 17s. was abstracted.
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s., and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310217-157]
584. GEORGE TWINING was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of December, 5 lbs. of nails, value 2s., the goods of William Philip Dammerham; and that he had before been convicted of felony.
JOSEPH KENDRICK. I apprehended the prisoner about five weeks ago, from information I had received; he was not carrying any thing - I had taken Costello into custody on the 22nd of December, and while I was taking him to the station, the prisoner addressed him - I desired the prisoner to go away, and threatened to handcuff him; he went away - I did not then know he had stolen any goods; I saw him afterwards, and knew him - I brought him in, and the prosecutor identified him.
WILLIAM PHILIP DAMMERHAM. I am a jobbing carpenter, and live in Monmouth-court, Seven-dials. I lost my nails on the 22nd of December; my wife came and told me the prisoner wanted to give me an order - I went home and saw him; he asked whether I would make him some boxes, and what price I would make them for - he was then in my shop; he was quite a stranger to me - I told him the price; he hesitated a good bit, and said he could make them cheaper himself - he was alone at that time; he then went away - I thought he had a bulk at his side, and I followed him between thirty and forty yards - I then saw him give a paper to Costello, who was about ten yards from the top of the court where I live; I believe the prisoner took the parcel from his side - the prisoner turned his head, saw me, and made off immediately: Costello put the parcel into his hat, and his hat on his head - he was taken, tried, and convicted - I had seen the nails on the end of my bench; there were six packages.
MARY DAMMERHAM. I am the wife of the prosecutor. I remember the prisoner coming to the shop - he asked if my husband was at home; I said No, but I would go and fetch him, which I did - the nails had been on the bench, and when he went they were missing; I know these are the nails - I left no one in the shop but my little girl and the prisoner; I was not absent more than two minutes - there were six papers of nails on the bench, and two of them were taken; we lost two thousand nails.
WILLIAM PHILIP DAMMERHAM. These are my nails; here is my own mark on the paper.
GEORGE TIMOTHY KIRTLAND. I am the beadle of St. Ann, Westminster. On Thursday, the 19th of May, in the sixth year of the late King, the prisoner was convicted for stealing two umbrellas and one coat, and was ordered to be transported for seven years - I attended the trial, and know he is the man.
GUILTY. Aged 52. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310217-158]
585. WILLIAM WALTON was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of February, 1 door-mat, value 5s., the goods of Abraham Lamert.
JOSEPH LAMERT. I am the son of Abraham Lamert - he is a patent medicine-vender. On the 9th of February I was sitting in our parlour, and was alarmed by my dog, who heard the prisoner in the passage - I got up, opened the door, and the mat was gone; I looked out, and saw the prisoner about four doors off with the mat on his back - I pursued him, brought him back, and sent for a Police-constable - I have seen the prisoner before, but did not know him; our front door is always open, and this mat was between that and the inner door.
GUILTY. Aged 55.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Three Weeks.

586. MARY WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 1 bonnet, value 1s.; 1 bag, value 1s., and 1 book, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Stillman, from the person of Hannah Amelia Stillman . (See page 262.)
SUSAN STILLMAN. I am the wife of Thomas Stillman; we live at No. 15, Sherrard-street, Golden-square - my daughter Hannah Amelia is five years old. On the 17th of January she had been at school the whole of the day, and ought to have returned at a quarter-past four o'clock, but she did not come till a quarter-past five, when she came without her bonnet, her bag, her book, and two or three other things - she was in a very fatigued state, and had been crying.
ANN HOLE. I keep the school to which this child comes - I tied on her bonnet at four o'clock, on the 17th of January, and sent her home with her bag and her book; my house is not a quarter of a mile from the prosecutor's.
ANN LOMAX. The prisoner came to my shop, No. 78, Monmouth-street, which is about fifty yards from the prosecutor's, on the 17th of January; she brought an old bonnet in her hand for sale; I asked what she wanted for it - she said 6d.; I said I could only sell it for that, and I offered her 4d. - I asked if it was her own; she said Yes, that her sister lived in a gentleman's family, and she should have many of these little things; this is the bonnet.
JOHN JAMES GANNELL. I am a Police-constable. I saw the prisoner on my beat on the 17th of January; I watched her for some time - she went into a marine-store shop, and offered a small jug for sale; the woman would not buy it, as she said she had offered a bonnet for sale - the prisoner said she had sold the bonnet for 4d.; I went and inquired about it, then ran after the prisoner, and took her; she threw the jug away, and broke it - I said I wanted her for a bonnet; she denied it, but I took her to the station, and there she was recognized.
SUSAN STILLMAN. This is my child 's bonnet.
GUILTY. Aged 31. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310217-160]
587. THOMAS BACCHUS was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of January, 1 pair of boots, value 5s., the goods of George Pygall.
SARAH PYGALL. I am the wife of George Pygall - he is a shoemaker. On the 27th of January I was in our parlour, at nine o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner leaning over the counter; he appeared to be reaching for something - I went up to him, and asked what he wanted; he made no answer - I said he should not go out till he told me what he had in his apron - he then said it was a pair of boots which he was going to mend; he opened his apron - I said he had taken them from the counter; he said I was wrong, he had bought them in coming down the street for 1s. 6d.; I detained him till my husband came, and then he said he had bought them in Holborn - my cousin came out of the parlour, and stood against the door till my husband came in.
WILLIAM MASTERS. I am the cousin of this witness - she took the boots from his apron; I came out of the parlour, shut the shop door, and detained the prisoner - he said he had them to mend.
JOHN GOODWIN. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I leave it to the mercy of the Court
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310217-161]
588. MARY CORBETT was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of January, 2 printed books, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Trueman.
THOMAS TRUEMAN. I live in Vine-street, and am overseer of the district of Saffron-hill - I am a bookseller. On the 20th of January the prisoner came to my shop - shesaid, "My son, whom you sent to the hospital, is dead;" I said, "I did not send any one to the hospital, but I think my brother overseer has;" she then said her two children at home were starving - I told her I would come down to her house; I then missed two books - the prisoner had then got into the passage; I called her back, lifted up her shawl, and found these two books, which are mine.
Prisoner. I did not go out of the shop; when he asked about them I said, "Here are two on the chair, perhaps these are them." Witness. No, it was not so - she had got out into the passage; I have since found she had no child dead.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not say he was dead then; it is eight months ago since he died.
WILLIAM HALL. I took the prisoner. She told me she had a son dead and her two children starving.
GUILTY. Aged 36. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-162]
589. JOHN MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 1 gown, value 4s.; 1 shift, value 4s., and 1 towel, value 6d., the goods of Martha Barker.
MARTHA BARKER. I am single, and live in Vine-street, Camden-town. The prisoner has lodged there three months; he had the back room on the first floor; a man his wife, and a little boy, live in the back parlour - the property stated was taken from the table drawer in the back parlour; the prisoner had no right there - I missed it on the 17th of January, but the prisoner had left on the 11th of January - I missed several other articles; I first missed the gown, and in seeking for that I discovered the property which he had pawned two months before; he came to me as a single man - he is a shoemaker, and might have plenty of work.
JOSEPH COOPER. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner; I found thirty-seven duplicates in his room; among them was one for this shift.
THOMAS PETO. I am shopman to Mr. Brickhill, a pawnbroker, in Tottenham-court-road. I have a shift and towel, pawned by the prisoner on the 15th of November; they are both claimed by the prosecutrix.
Prisoner's Defence. I left there, and went to No. 5, Little Camden-street; the prosecutrix came and said they wanted to see my room; I let them in, and they took me - I owed them a trifle of money, and I pawned the articles for leather to make some shoes - her son used to sleep in the same room when he was at home.
MARTHA BARKER. These are my articles - he did owe me 9s. 6d.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Confined Six Months.

OLD COURT. TUESDAY, FEBRUARY 22.
First London Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
590. GEORGE WINGROVE was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 tub, value 2s.; and 112lbs. of white lead, value 30s., the goods of James Crease and another.
CHARLES WHITAKER. I live in Maidenhead-court, Aldersgate-street, with my father, who is an auctioneer's porter. On Thursday evening last, about a quarter to seven o'clock, I saw the prisoner at Mr. Crease' door, in Smithfield, with two other men - I saw one of the other men help a tub of white lead on the prisoner's shoulder, just at the end of the rails outside the shop - the prisoner ran away across Smithfield with it, and the others went a different way - I went into the shop, and told what I had seen; I and Crease's man went after the prisoner - I did not see him stopped; I saw him afterwards, and am certain he is the man I saw run away with the cask - he was brought back with it; I saw him before the Alderman next day, and was certain of him then.
BENJAMIN CATMULL. I am a constable. I was going through Smithfield on Thursday evening, about a quarter to seven o'clock - the prisoner passed me with a keg on his back; I suspected something wrong, and followed him; he crossed from Crease' shop, and was about seventy yards from it; I followed him into Bartholomew-close, and while I was watching where he was going, Welch came running up with Whitaker, and in consequence of what he said I secured the prisoner, without losing sight of him - Welch claimed the keg as his master's I am sure I never lost sight of him.
Prisoner. That is false, he did.
STEPHEN WELCH. I am in the employ of James and Orlando Crease, oilmen, of Smithfield; there are no other partners. I had seen this cask of white lead about a quarter of an hour before the alarm; it was on the pavement at the door, and had just been brought out of a cart - Whitaker gave an alarm; I went out with him, and saw the prisoner through Cloth-fair; I stopped him in Barley Mow-passage - Catmull, who was looking after him in Cloth-fair, had asked me what was the matter; I took hold of the keg on his shoulder, and Catmull seized him; I have no doubt of the keg - it is here, and contains white lead: there was no mark on it; I missed one from the door when the boy gave the alarm, and I had seen it a quarter of an hour before - there had been eight at the door.
CHARLES WHITAKER. I saw the prisoner with the keg when I went into Cloth-fair, which was not more than five minutes after I saw him take it from the door.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going along Smithfield, five hundred yards from the house; a man came up, asked me to carry this to Barbican, and he would satisfy me - this man came up, and said, "You have my property," and gave me in charge; the officer asked if he was sure it was his; he said he did not know that it was, and at the Compter he said it was not his.
STEPHEN WELCH. I never said it was not my master's - I attended before the Magistrate, but I could not swear to it, as I did not see it taken from the door.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-164]
591. WILLIAM EDWARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 mat, value 1s., the goods of George Spencer and another.
ANN FENDICK. I am housekeeper to George Spencer and another, solicitor s, Size-lane. There were two mats in their passage; one was chained to the door, and the other sewn to it. On Thursday last, about half-past five o'clock, I heard the chain make a noise, as if it was disturbed; I was in the kitchen; I ran up, and saw theprisoner with the mat in his hands, upright - I am certain he is the man; I said, "Oh! you thief," he dropped it, and walked away - he had separated it from the one which was chained - I went out after him, and I never lost sight of him; I laid hold of his coat; he broke from me, but was stopped by a gentleman - it was in the daytime.
MR. CHARLES AUGUSTUS ROBERTS. I am a merchant, and live in Bucklersbury - I saw the prisoner and witness disputing, and saw the prisoner break from her; she desired me to pursue, as he was a thief - he walked to the corner of Size-lane, then ran off; I followed, and stopped him - Fendick came up, and charged him with stealing the mat; he begged to be let go - I did not lose sight of him.
RICHARD GEORGE STATHAM. I am a constable - I received him in charge, and on his person found a screwdriver, a bag, a large knife, and two small screws - here is the mat; the string it is sewn with is cut.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. When the woman came out I was twenty yards from the door; she said, "You wretch, what have you been doing?" I said I had done no harm, and went round the corner; the gentleman laid hold of me - I have been out of employ a long time, and many days have not broken my fast for nine hours.
GUILTY. Aged 33. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-165]
592. JOHN HARDING was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 12 ozs. of copper, value 6d.; 3 weights, value 1s. 6d., and 17 copper coins, value 3d., the goods of John Warner and another, his masters.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
HENRY MILWARD. I am in the employ of John and Robert Warner, brass-founder s, Jewin-crescent - the prisoner was in their employ. On the 17th of January, in consequence of directions, I watched him, and about a quarter-past twelve o'clock I saw him take the copper out of the pan and put it into his pocket - it was old halfpence and pocket-pieces intended for melting; I told Mr. Handley.
JAMES WILLIAM HANDLEY. I am foreman to Messrs. Warner. In consequence of what Milward said, I fetched the prisoner from the foundry into the counting-house, and told him he had something in his possession which did not belong to him - he said he had not; I said he had - he pulled out two halfpence; I said, "That will not do, I must have the rest" - he then pulled out 12 ozs. of small copper, said it was the first time, and he hoped I would let him go; I went with the officer to his lodging, and found a 1 lb., a 2 lbs., and a - lb. weight, and a piece of copper, which are masters'.
Cross-examined by MR. STURGEON. Q. Is it unusual for men when melting to put money into their pockets? A. No, it is never allowed.
JOHN ANDREWS. I produce the copper, which was delivered to me.
JAMES WILLIAM HANDLEY. This is the copper I delivered to the constable - we have a hundred and forty men in our employ.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you hold out any threat or promise to him? A. None at all - he has a wife and six children; I believe master had a fifteen years' character with him.
JURY. Q. What did he earn a week? A. For the last twelve weeks he has earned 30s. a week.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-166]
593. HENRY KILLMINSTER was indicted for a misdemeanor.
MR. ELLIS conducted the prosecution.
JOSHUA GRIFFITH. I am servant to John Drake, who keeps a coal-shed, No. 7, Lambeth-hill. On the 21st of January, about five o'clock in the evening, the prisoner came for 2d. worth of fire-wood - it was dark; he offered me a half-crown, which I took, and gave him 2s. 4d. change - he went away; I took the half-crown in to mistress, left it with her, and in about a quarter of an hour I saw him on Lambeth-hill, in Collier's custody - my master said to me in his hearing, "Is this the man who gave you the half-crown?" I said Yes, it was - he said he had never been near our shop; I am certain he is the man.
MARY ANN DRAKE. I am the wife of John Drake. On the 21st of January, about five o'clock, I received a half-crown from Griffith - I had received no other that evening; I remarked in a minute or two that it was very had, and put it into a corner of the desk on a book - there was no other half-crown near it; I was particular in keepit by itself - in ten minutes or a quarter of an hour Masters came to change a half-crown; I thought it looked like the one I had from Griffith - I called my husband, who went away with her, and in five or ten minutes Collier brought the prisoner in; I gave him the same half-crown as I had from Griffith - I took it off the book where I had put it; I can swear it is the same.
HANNAH MASTERS. My husband is in the Guards, and I keep a green-shop at No. 25, Lambeth-hill. On the 21st of January, at a quarter-past five o'clock, the prisoner was outside my window - I was in my room; he said, "Halloo, mistress;" I went out to him, but in the mean time two other men passed, and he said to them, "Two or three?" they said, "Get three;" they went on, and he stopped, put his hand on a savoy, and asked the price - I said 1 1/2d. - he only asked for one; I served him - he had 3 lbs. of potatoes for 2d., which made 3 1/2d.; he gave me half a crown, which I could not change, and went two doors below, to Mrs. Buckle's - she weighed the half-crown, and it was light - it never went out of my sight; I then went to Drake's, and in consequence of what passed there, she called her husband, and the prisoner was apprehended by Collier, to whom I gave the half-crown, but cut a piece out of it before I parted with it; Griffith said, in his hearing, that he had given him a half-crown - the prisoner said he had not been near his shop.
JOSEPH COLLIER. I apprehended the prisoner about five o'clock that day, waiting at Masters' door for his change; I took him into Drake's parlour, and searched him, but found no money on him - he had three bundles of wood in his apron; I received this half-crown from Masters, and another from Drake.
JOHN FIELD. I am inspecter of counterfeit coin for the Mint. Both these half-crowns are counterfeit - they are made of Britannia metal, and appear cast from the samemould; the metal is much lighter than silver - the coin of the Realm is struck with a die.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that a man named Clark had met him, and sent him with the money, which he did not know was counterfeit.
GUILTY.
Confined Six Months, and to find Sureties.
[trialAccount t18310217-167]
594. WILLIAM FERGUSSON was indicted for a misdemeanor.
THOMAS BEVAN. I am a surgeon, and live in Queen-street, Cheapside. I know Job Grimshaw, of Staples Inn-buildings, a bookbinder - I cannot identify the prisoner's person; but this paper was brought to me, and I delivered to the person who brought it, three sovereigns, for Mr. Grimshaw, on the 27th of December, believing that the paper came from Grimshaw, who had bound books for me-I saw the prisoner at the Mansion-house about three weeks after, charged with this offence - (paper read.)
27th of December, 1830.
SIR, - I take the liberty of writing to you to ask the favor of the loan of 3l. for a few days; if you can oblige I shall be glad.
Yours, J. GRIMSHAW.
JOB GRIMSHAW. I live at No. 6, Staples Inn-buildings, and am acquainted with Thomas Bevan. The prisoner was in my service about nine months, and had left about eighteen months when this occurred; I believe while he was with me I sent him to Mr. Bevan's on business - I did not send him on the 27th of December, or any body, to request the loan of 3l. from Mr. Bevan; this paper is not written by me, nor by my authority - the prisoner never brought me the 3l.; I did not know he was going on such an errand.
HENRY CLEMENTS. I am assistant to Messrs. Hooper and Bevan, of Queen-street. On the 27th of December I recollect the prisoner calling on Mr. Bevan with a note - it was not sealed, and I opened it; it requested the loan of 3l.; when Mr. Bevan came in he read it, went up stairs, and brought the prisoner down a small brown paper parcel - I am certain of his person, but not positive of the date; it was about that time - I saw him in custody at the Mansion-house about three weeks after, and was quite certain of him.
JURY. Q. Did you ask the boy where he came from? A. The moment he brought it he said he came from Job Grimshaw, and it requested an answer; he did not say where Grimshaw lived.
MR. BEVAN. I gave him the sovereigns in brown paper.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Seven Years.

595. BENJAMIN MILLS was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of January, 1 cloak, value 5s., the goods of Joseph Markham.
JOSEPH MARKHAM. I live in Williams'-mews, Hampstead-road, and am driver of the cab, No. 194. On the 18th of January I went into a chemist's-shop, No. 260, Tottenham-court-road, and left my cab at the door, with a cloak on the seat; I saw the prisoner pass, and take the cloak - I ran out; he turned into Russell-street, and threw it down - I took it up, and followed him; I lost sight of him for about a minute; when I saw him again he was still running - I have no doubt that he is the man; I took him - the Police-constable came and took him; he said he was very sorry for it.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Was any body with you when he made this statement? A. The Policeman was; it was not a very dark night - it was between ten and eleven o'clock; he was taken within one hundred yards of the spot.
JOSEPH STANNARD. I am a Police-constable. I heard a cry of Stop thief! I ran, saw the prisoner running very fast, and a number of people after him, up Russell-street, Tavistock-street, and round into Tottenham-court-road - he told me he had had no victuals the whole of the day, and he would starve before he would do any thing of the kind again.
Cross-examined. Q. He seemed very contrite? A. Yes - he was quite cut up about it.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18310217-169]
596. HENRY JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 pair of trousers, value 7s., the goods of Jonas Benjamin.
MICHAEL GABRIEL. I live in Homer-street, Marylebone, and am a dealer in clothes. I was at my door between ten and eleven o'clock on the 16th of February, and saw the prisoner handling some trousers at Jonas Benjamin 's window - I thought he handled them rather longer than he ought, and I saw him unloop a pair, put them under his arm, and walk down the street: I followed, and as he turned the corner of Crawford-street, I tapped him on the shoulder - I took him back; a mob gathered round - he endeavored to get away, but I gave him in charge.
THOMAS HENRY THOMPSON. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner outside Mr. Benjamin's shop: I asked how he came in possession of the trousers - he said he had picked them up.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going down the street, and there were several Jews going by, with loads of clothes - I saw the trousers on the pavement; I took them up, and walked quietly down the street - the witness said he would keep me; a number of people came up, said he was no officer, and had no right to hold me, so I tried to put his hands off.
JURY to MICHAEL GABRIEL. Q. Did he bring the same trousers back which you saw him take from the shop? A. Yes, these are them - they have the mark on them.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-170]
597. JOSEPH CARMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of January, 1 cap, value 4s., the goods of William Reynolds, the elder, from the person of William Reynolds, the younger; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
WILLIAM REYNOLDS, JUN. I am the son of William Reynolds - he lives in Freeman's-street, Spital fields, and is a shoemaker. On Monday, the 24th of January, I was sent on an errand to Long-alley - I had to pass through Dorset-street; I saw the prisoner and some other boy s there; the prisoner came up to my side, snatched my cap off, and gave it to his companions - they then ran away, towardsFrench-alley; the other boys called the prisoner Joe - I was sent for to the Police-station on the Friday night, where I saw the prisoner, and knew him; I am quite clear he is the person who took my cap - I had seen him before; it was a new seal skin cap.
JAMES WALTON. I am a Police-constable. I received information, and looked for the prisoner, and on the Friday night I met him in White Lion-street, at half-past eight o'clock, with three or four other boys; he saw me, and ran towards Shoreditch - I went up to the corner of White Lion-street, and he was peeping round the corner; he then ran down Shoreditch - I pursued, and took him into custody; he said, "What do you want me for?" I said for a boy's cap which he had stolen on the Monday - he said, "Let me go home to my mother," but I took him to the station.
SARAH REYNOLDS. I am the wife of William Reynolds. On this night my son had a new seal skin cap on, which I gave 4s. 6d. for - the next morning, when I was going to send him out, he said he had lost it, but he should get it back again, as he knew the boy.
Prisoner's Defence. I can only say I know nothing of the cap.
THOMAS COTTERELL. I am a Police-constable. I produce a certificate of a former conviction of the prisoner in May last - I attended, and know he is the person.
GUILTY. Aged 12. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310217-171]
597. MARY STEVENS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 1 watch, value 30s., the goods of Thomas Martin, from his person.
THOMAS MARTIN. I live at Little Stanmore, and was a farmer, but the lease of the land is out. I went to the Red Lion public-house, at Paddington, about seven o'clock, and stopped till a little after eight; I saw the prisoner there - I was not so much in liquor but that I knew what I was about; I am quite clear I saw the prisoner, and I think she had a glass of gin at my expence, but whether she drank it or not I do not know - I saw two other women there; I did not get more intoxicated - I went from there to the Key in Bell-square; after a while the prisoner came in - I was then going from there to a man's house, whom I wanted to see; the prisoner followed me, and snatched my watch out of my pocket - I do not know the name of the street; it is at the lower end of Bell-street - when she caught the watch out of my fob I was knocked down by three or four persons, and who had the watch I cannot tell, but I am clear the prisoner took it; it was not light - I could not tell the men, but there were three or four of them; I suppose it might be nine o'clock - I do not think there were any lamps - not to give much light there; but I know the prisoner - she followed me out of the Key, but she was a distance behind; I did not see her follow me, but I know her person - I have seen her many scores of times, for years past at Paddington; upon my oath she is the person who took my watch - I lost my purse and all my money, which was torn out of my pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. You are quite sure she is the person who took your watch? A. Yes; I was picked up by the Policeman - she was taken the next day; I swear I told the Policeman who picked me up, that it was the prisoner, but I did not know her name - I was not so drunk when I went to the Red Lion but that I could do any business, and I knew what I was about; I did not sit down there - I had drank at several different places where I had called; most likely I had had some gin - I might have had rum, but no brandy; that is a liquor I seldom drink - I cannot tell how many public-houses I had been in; I should think I had not been in half a dozen, but I could not swear to a thing like that, or to what I had there - I had no beer at the Red Lion to my recollection; I was not in there five minutes - very likely somebody might give me some beer; I cannot say whether I had three several quarterns of gin at the Red Lion, or what I had - the place was full of people all the time; I did not go away from the Red Lion with the prisoner that I know of - I never saw her go out; I went away by myself, and went to the Key; I wanted to find a man there -I cannot say whether I had treated the prisoner at the Red Lion; I and another man were tossing up, and whether she had any gin or not I cannot say - I cannot tell what I drank at the Key; I think it was gin - I do not think I had more than a glass; I had no wine there - I had none to my knowledge; I cannot swear what I drank in the course of the day - I did not go away with the prisoner from the Key; I know the prisoner came in there - I think I lost my watch about nine o'clock; it was past seven before I went to the Red Lion: I did not state the next morning that I was so drunk I could not tell whether the prisoner was the person or not - I always said it was her; I had no other persons taken up but her; there were two men taken, but I did not take them - there were three or four men in the crowd.
WILLIAM ELLIOTT. I am a Police-constable. From information I received I went to Burn-street, Lisson-grove, on Monday, the 17th of January, the night the robbery was said to have happened - I found the prisoner about half-past eleven o'clock, sitting in a room before the fire, on the floor; she was nearly naked - there were two men sitting one on each side of the fire; I went up to one of the men named King, who was sitting with his head down, apparently asleep - I put my hand on his head, and he looked up in my face - I said, "Where is the watch?" he said,"What watch, master?" I said, "The old man's watch;" he said, "I know nothing about it;" I said, "Then I shall take you all into custody;" the prisoner said, "Then, so help me - , if you do you will take the wrong;" a great many angry words passed, saying they would not go - the prisoner then turned to the other man, named Collins; she put her hand on his knee, and said, "Never mind Jem, I will cut this bl-y throat of mine before I will split," and he answered, "Never mind Poll, they shall, or I will, put a rope round this bl-y neck of mine before I will split;" we were obliged to get the assistance of four more constables, before we could get them away - I took them all to the station, and as we were going, I heard the prisoner say to a Mr. Hainsworth, "Never mind, you may say what you like, I know I shall get lagged."
Cross-examined. Q. Did she appear to have been drinking? A. She had been drinking.
Prisoner's Defence. I was half naked - I had a blue striped gown on, which I could not get off without taking it over my shoulders; I objected going - I told him if hewould bring the person who had lost his watch, I would go quietly; a person came to me from a person who used to cohabit with Hainsworth, saying that she knew who had the watch, and I sent a note to the governor about it.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310217-172]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
598. WILLIAM ROBROUGH GORNALL was indicted for bigamy.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
OCTAVIUS YOUNG THISELTON. I am parish-clerk of St. Pancras. I have the register of marriages in that church. On the 17th of July, 1823, William Gornall and Eleanor Elizabeth Prince were married there.
ISAAC KIMPTON. I am an officer of St. Pancras. I know a woman who applied for relief, in the name of Mrs. Gornall, two or three months ago - I saw the prisoner with her; she got a warrant against him, and he was taken into custody - she claimed in his presence to be his wife; he said he was very sorry for what had happened - he acknowledged she was his wife.
Q. Did he deny that she was his wife in any way? A. No.
Cross-examined by MR. BALL. Q. Did he never state to you, that there was another William Gornall, a cousin of his, who was a mariner? A. Never, I am quite sure; I saw the prisoner and the woman I called his wife together, in Britannia-street, when I apprehended them - this conversation took place in the street, in going to Hatton-garden Office.
COURT. Q. Did both the wives claim relief from the parish? A. No, the first only.
HARRY PRICE. I am overseer of St. Pancras. A woman claiming to be the wife of the prisoner, applied for relief - I gave orders for a warrant to be issued against the prisoner; he was brought to the vestry-room - I saw him there with Isaac Kimpton; I said I thought it was a great pity the parish should be burdened with his family - he said he had allowed her 10s. a week for some time, but was unable to do so then; I said he must go before the Magistrate, which he did - his wife was there and identified him as her husband; the Magistrate remanded him -I applied to the parish-clerk of All Souls, Marylebone, and got a certificate of the register, which I here produce; I examined it, and it is correct. - (read)
"In the district rectory of All Souls, Marylebone, William Robrough Gornall, batchelor, and Hannah Palmer, spinster, were married by banns, on the 4th of October, 1829, by me, H. Nathan, curate, in the presence of Jane Watkins and William Watkins."
Cross-examined. Q. Are you not confounding Hannah Palmer with the other wife? A. No - they were both present at the two examinations, but I am speaking of the first time; he was then speaking of his wife, not of Hannah Palmer - he certainly did not speak about Hannah Palmer when he spoke of his wife; he did not tell me that Eleanor Elizabeth was the wife of a cousin of his, who was a mariner at sea, and that he had allowed her 10s. a week for him.
HANNAH PALMER. I married the prisoner at All Souls, Marylebone, on the 4th of October, 1829.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had cohabited with the woman supposed to be his first wife, but that she was the wife of William Gornall, who was now at sea; and that he had done his best to maintain both her and Palmer He received a good character.
HANNAH PALMER re-examined. Q. Whose child is that in your arms? A. The prisoner's - I had been living at home with my father, before I married him; he never represented that he was married, and I never suspected it - he was an excellent husband to me, and a good father.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-173]
599. JANE ROGERS was indicted for bigamy.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
HENRY FOLKES. I produce a certificate of the marriage of Jane Nowell and James Rogers, at St. Ann's, Westminster, on the 26th of May, in the 42nd year of the reign of George the Third.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Where did you get it? A. From Mr. Sawyer; I saw the register, and can take upon me to say that this is a literal transcript of it.
WILLIAM NORTON. I am a chair-maker, and live at No. 11, Carnaby-street. I knew the prisoner in 1802, and before that; she was then single - I was invited to go to her wedding, in that year, but I could not go - she was to be married to a person of the name of Rogers; I had known him before - I think the wedding was about May; her master gave an entertainment, but I was not there - I think I saw James Rogers the next day, but the prisoner was not there; Rogers is alive, and is outside the door - he allowed her ten shillings a week, for some time, and I stopped the payment of it; her name was Jane Nowell before her marriage - she afterwards went by the name of Rogers.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you know her before? A. Yes, by coming to see her sister - I was invited to the wedding by her brother-in-law, who gave her away; I do not know that she is out of her mind - I am told her husband has paid for her, as a lunatic pauper, in the parish of Clerkenwell; I had known Rogers perhaps two years before - I did not know that he had been married before.
JOHN HUNTER MARLER. I married the woman at the bar, on the 31st of March, 1807.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you know Rogers? A. No; I have paid eight shillings a week for the prisoner, for about two years - I did send her to a mad-house, but I was with her for fourteen or fifteen years, till I became in danger of my life. and I was advised to part with her; she has been living as a lunatic pauper in Clerkenwell.
GUILTY. Aged 49. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310217-174]
600. FREDERICK SCAMMELL was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of January, 7 boxes, value 7s., the goods of James Tilt; and JOHN SHEPHERD was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing it to have been stolen; against the Statute, &c.
MARY REEVE. I have lately been nursing in the family of Mr. James Tilt, a tobacconist. On the 19th of January, fifteen or sixteen snuff-boxes were taken from his window; they could be taken by a person without coming into the shop.
THOMAS NISBETT. I live with my father and mother,in Ravenhill's-rents, near Leather-lane. About the middle of last month, I saw the two prisoners together in Leather-lane, between seven and eight o'clock in the evening; I had known them before - I saw Scammell sell some boxes to Shepherd; they were in Scammell's hand when I first saw them - he sold them to Shepherd; I believe I saw five of them - I believe Shepherd said he would give 9d. for them, but I saw no money pass; I did not see any shilling, nor heard any thing about change for a shilling - no one has been talking to me lately about this; I told my mother that I saw Shepherd buy the boxes; I did not see him put them into his apron.
WILLIAM ATTRIDGE. I am a Police-constable. I received information from Nisbett that he had seen some boxes pass between the prisoners; I took Scammell first, and asked him what he had done with the boxes which he had - he said he had had no boxes; I mentioned about some being taken from a tobacconist's in Gray's Inn-lane - he still denied it; I then said he had sold some to Shepherd - he said he had sold him five for 9d.: I sent for Shepherd, who denied having any, but when I told him Scammell had acknowledged he had sold him some, he said he had bought five of him; he then took me to a house, and gave me five, and when I got him to the station he told me there were two more at his house - I went there and got them; he sells greens and other articles about the streets.
Shepherd. Q. Did you ever see me do any thing wrong, or idle my time? A. No.
JAMES TILT. These are my boxes. I put eighteen boxes on one side of my window that morning, and eighteen on the other; I lost fifteen of them in the evening - a piece of glass was broken out of my window; it had been mended, but the piece was quite firm in - these are worth seven shillings.
Scammell's Defence. Shepherd bought them not knowing them to be stolen; I told him I had found them.
Shepherd's Defence. I bought them, but I declare I never knew they were stolen.
SCAMMELL - GUILTY. Aged 13.
Transported for Seven Years.
SHEPHERD - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310217-175]

EDWARD BONIFACE. I am a cheesemonger. The prisoner received a good character, and a gentleman stated he would take him into his employ.

602. ANN WILLIAMS and ELLEN HOLLAND were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of February, 2 sheets, value 1s. 3d., and 1 quilt, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Philip Ward.
KEZIAH WARD. I am the wife of William Ward; we live at No. 9, New-way, Westminster. On the 4th of February Williams came to my house alone, and wanted a room for herself and another young woman; she said they got their bread by needle-work - I took her up stairs, and showed her the second floor front-room which was furnished; she took it and gave me a shilling earnest - they were to give 4s. a week for it; they took the room immediately, but I did not see Holland till I went up stairs at night to give them a pair of sheets and a counterpane - they were then in the room; they continued to live there till the 9th of February; when I went up and knocked at the door they asked who was there - I said the landlady, and I wanted to come in for something particular; they said they were cleaning themselves, but if I would go down stairs they would call me when they were ready - I stood on the landing-place; when the door was opened I looked in and missed the bed, and saw the room was stripped of nearly every thing but the table and a tea-kettle - they got down stairs; I called out, but nobody could get out of their rooms in time to prevent them - they got into the street; we called the Policeman - they were pursued and taken; I missed the saucepain, pail, fire-shovel, and a variety of other things.
THOMAS COX. I am in the service of Mr. Ravenhill, a pawnbroker. I have two sheets; one pawned on the 5th, and the other on the 7th of February, by one of the prisoners, but I cannot call to recollection which, though I know their faces - the articles are both in the name of Mary Williams, but with two different directions.
WILLIAM MUGGERIDGE. I am in the service of Mr. Harris, a pawnbroker. I have a quilt, pawned by Williams, in the name of Ann Holland, on the 7th of February.
Williams. It was not me; I sent it - I had not a gown to go out in. Witness. I am quite certain it was her.
JOHN BENTON. I am a Police-constable. I was at the apprehension of both these girls; when they were in the watch-house I went to the room they had lived in, and found in a box these two duplicates of the sheets - when they were before the Magistrate they voluntarily said they had pawned them to get bread.
THOMAS COX. These are the duplicates I gave them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoners put in a paper, stating that they were in a very destitute condition, and that the prosecutrix kept a house of ill fame.
WILLIAM - GUILTY. Aged 19.
HOLLAND - GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Seven Years.

603. EDWARD HERBERT was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 1 cap, value 5s., the goods of Joseph Jarrett, from the person of Frederick Jarrett ; and EDWARD GILES was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing it to have been stolen.
JOSEPH JARRETT. I am eleven years old, and live with my father and mother at Chelsea. On the 13th of February I was in my father's bed-room, and saw my little brother Frederick run round the house into College-place; he had his cap on then; the two prisoners followed him -I saw Herbert take the cap off his head, give it to Giles, and they both ran off together down John-street; I went and told my father - he told the Police-constable; they were taken about eleven o'clock at night - I know theyare the same persons; I observed them for a quarter of an hour at the window - the cap has never been found.
FREDERICK JARRETT. I am eight years old. I went out; Herbert took my cap off, and gave it to the other -I am sure they are the men; I have not found the cap.
JOSEPH JARRETT. I am the father of these two boy s. I did not see the prisoners near my house; my sons described them, and recognized them the same night.
GEORGE CASLEY. I am an officer. The mother of these lads described the prisoners to me: I took Herbert at the corner of Manor-street, King's-road - he behaved very well; I took Giles in the Lord Nelson public-house, a little before ten o'clock - I have not been able to find the cap; Herbert bears an excellent character from his late master.
COURT to JOSEPH JARRETT. Q. At the time your brother's cap was taken off by Herbert, was Giles close to him? A. Yes.
HERBERT - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
GILES - NOT GUILTY.

605. JOHN ATTERBURY was indicted for embezzlement.
ROBERT COX. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Tothill-street, Westminster. The prisoner came into my employ on the 9th of December, as a porter, and to serve behind the counter occasionally - he was employed to receive monies for me; he had conducted himself with property.
GEORGE DENHAM. I am in the prosecutor's service. On the 12th of February I saw the prisoner take a half-crown from a customer; he stooped down, and put it into his boot - I was in the shop to watch him; I had seen him do this twice in the course of the evening - I think the customer bought 2 lbs. of one shilling butter; he gave 6d. out of the till - his boot was examined; three half-half-crowns were found on him there, and one at the watch-house - he said he did not know he had any money there.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. Where were you? A. At the head of the counter - I do not remember what customer it was; I can swear they bought butter, and I believe it was about 2 lbs.; he only said he did not know there was any thing in his boot - he did not say that if there was it must have got through a hole in his trousers - he said so after his examination - I did not observe whether his trousers came over his boots, but they were laced boots, and came up three or four inches.
DANIEL REARDON. On the night stated Mr. Cox called me in: I went into the parlour behind the shop, and found the prisoner - I searched him, and found three half-crowns in a pocket on the side of his waistcoat - he said, "They are my own; I did not take them, Mr. Cox;" I took him to the office, and in the back room I said to him, "Have you any more about you?" he said No; I said, "Pull off your boots" - he pulled off one very gently, and said, "You see there is nothing there;" I took it, and found another half-crown in the toe of it - he then said they had fallen from the till.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-180]

607. MARY HALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of January, 1 bonnet, value 15s., and 1 cloak, value 15s., the goods of Ann Bradley.
ANN BRADLEY. I am a servant, at No. 23, Grosvenor-cottages. On the 9th of January I was out of a situation, and lodged in the same room with the prisoner - about six o'clock that evening I lost my bonnet and cloak from the room; the bonnet was found on the prisoner's head; the cloak I have not seen - the prisoner went away without notice, and she was taken on the 22nd of January.
DANIEL REEDON. I am a labourer. I slept in another bed in the same room - I saw the prisoner go out with the cloak and bonnet on; I laid on the bed, and she went out - I did not know whose they were them.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you know she had lent me them? A. No.
CHARLES MAYHEW. I am an officer. I took the priprisoner on the 22nd of January, in St. Giles'; I had information that she had stolen these things about a week before - I took her to where she had lodged, and the occupier of the house said it was the prosecutor's bonnet; the prisoner said she was going to return it - I asked what she had done with the cloak, and she gave me no answer.
ANN BRADLEY. This is my bonnet - it was a dark brown cloth cloak, for which I gave a guinea; I never permitted her to take and wear my things - she was a stranger; I paid her lodging - I do not know what her trade is.
Prisoner. Yes you know very well I am an unfortunate girl, and have supported you and your child; have you not lent me that shawl you now have on? Witness. Yes, I did one night.
Prisoner's Defence. She lent me that shawl about six o'clock in the evening, and said she was distressed for clothes - I brought her in some money; she said, "As it is cold take my cloak and bonnet;" I was taken up that night, and had fourteen days in the House of Correction -I was taken by this officer the day I came out.
CHARLES MAYHEW. She told me the reason she had not returned the bonnet was, because she had been taken into custody.
NOT GUILTY.

JOHN THOMAS DAWRENCE. I was at the prosecutor's. I ran out, and saw this girl with the pork in a basket, which was her own, about a hundred yards from the gate.

609. MARY McLEAN was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of January, 4 yards of carpet, value 10s., the goods of Jessaint McCall Knapp and Frederick Augustus Knapp.
JOHN BAGULEY. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Oxford-street. On the 15th of January, about ten o'clock in the evening. the prisoner offered this piece of carpet to pledge, and stated it was the property of a person in Wardour-street - I suspected her from her rude behaviour, and sent for an officer.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Are you quite sure it was on the 15th of January? A. It was on a Saturday evening, the 15th of January.
JESSAINT McCALL KNAPP. I am in partnership with my brother Frederick August us Knapp; we are upholsterer s. The prisoner first came to work at our place in September, and she has worked there almost continually ever since - she received her wages between six and seven o'clock on the 15th of January, but she was not discharged; I expected her to return on Monday: this is our carpet - there are four yards of it; it is worth 14s. - I did not permit her to take it away.
Cross-examined. Q. She was not regularly employed by you? A. She was almost - I paid her her wages in the parlour on the Saturday night - I did not see her leave the house; I had seen this carpet in the course of that week -I cannot say what day; we had more of this pattern in the house, and some of it had been sold - I will not say positively when I had seen this piece; I did not miss it till the Tuesday, when I received information.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Judgment Respited .

610. LEONARD JAMES MITCHELL was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of January, 50 yards of cotton, value 4l., the goods of George Washington England, his master; and ELIZABETH MITCHELL was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing it to have been stolen.
GEORGE WASHINGTON ENGLAND. I am an upholsterer, and live in Welbeck-street. Leonard James Mitchell was my errand-boy for about three weeks previous to the 15th of January - I discharged him on a Monday, I suppose about the 12th or the 14th, having missed various little trifling articles; after he was gone I missed fifty yards of cotton, which are in whole pieces, with the exception of one, which has had a yard or two taken off by the maker.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Have you any partner? A. No; this young woman had come to my house after the boy had left - I had found fault with some part of his conduct; I did tap him on the head with a child's boot - he did not threaten to take out a warrant for me.
COURT. Q. Do you know whether his sister visited him while he was there? A. She came once or twice to the shop, and she came after he was gone to know why he had left, and wished he might return.
JAMES STRINGER. I live with Mr. Gofton, a pawnbroker. I have some cotton pawned by the female prisoner on the 7th of January.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you known her before? A. She had pawned some things, and released them again.
EBENEZER SOPER. I am a dealer in these articles - I have a mark on this cotton; I sold it to the prosecutor.
MR. ENGLAND. I believe this to be mine, but I have no mark on it.
NOT GUILTY.

JAMES CHURCH. I am a foot-patrol. 612. HENRY SCARLETT was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of January, 2 books, value 16s., the goods of Samuel Daniel Ewins.
JANE HEATH. I live shop-woman to Mr. Samuel Daniel Ewins, a bookseller, of Duke-street, Smithfield. On the 20th of January the prisoner, who was a stranger, came and said he wished to look at some books - I showed him some, and he told me to send them to a Mrs. Jones, No. 20, Barbican; he did not state his own name - I sent them there by the boy; the prisoner left, and as soon as he was gone I missed two books - I afterwards heard he was in custody: these books are Mr. Ewins'.
HENRY HALL. I am shopman to Mr. Attenborough, a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned these two books on the 20th of January - he came again on the 22nd, andoffered another book; I detained him - these are the books, they are two Bibles and one Prayer-book.
WILLIAM ATTFIELD. I was fetched to the pawnbroker's shop, and took the prisoner - I asked him how he came by the books he had pawned there; he said he had bought them in a large purchase of Mr. George Brown, of Birmingham, two years ago, but I found by the Prayer-book that it could not have been so, as it contains matter relative to the present Royal Family - I tied the prisoner's hands; he tried by all possible means to get away.
ELEANOR GROUTAGE. This Prayer-book is mine.
Prisoner's Defence. These are pot what I got at Birmingham - I bought these in the street.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
