674. JOHN COWDALL was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 2 shrubs, value 1s.; 30 wooden staves, value 1s., and 1lb. of glue, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Pickford and others, his masters.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM TIBBETTS. I am clerk to Thomas Pickford and others. The prisoner was in their employ - I saw him go out on the 16th of February, with a laurel shrub; I said nothing to him, but spoke to the head clerk; and on the prisoner's return, between six and seven o'clock, an officer, who was waiting, took him into custody - I went to his house in City-gardens; the officer asked where the trees were which he took from the wharf - he made no anwer, but went to the cellar, pulled out the two trees, and gave them to the officer; we found these staves in the cellar - he acknowledged taking them.
Cross-examined by MR. STURGEON. Q. Did you know the trees? A. No - we had a quantity on the wharf; he has been five or six years in our employ.
JAMES HANDLEY. I am an officer. I went with Tibbetts - the prisoner produced the property from the cellar.
Cross-examined. Q. What did you say to him? A. I said, "Where did those staves come from?" he said, "I am sorry to say I took them from the wharf."
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18310217-249]
675. DENNIS BRYAN was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of January, 1 watch, value 2l., the goods of James Sweeting, from his person.
HENRY CLARK. I am a locksmith. On the evening of the 21st of January, between eleven and twelve o'clock, I saw a young man, who appeared a sailor, in Ratcliff-highway - I saw the prisoner and two other men watching him - the prisoner was in the middle; he ran out from the others, and took the watch from the sailor - he went on; a private watchman met him, and struggled with him - I saw him drop the watch; the watchman sprang his rattle - a Policeman came up, and he was secured; I went before the Magistrate - the prosecutor attended, and said his name was James Sweeting; he is now gone to sea - I am sure I saw the prisoner take it out of his fob.
Prisoner. Q. Did you come up while the watchman had me? A. Yes, I came up immediately.
RICHARD JOHN ROBERTS. I am a private watchman. I was on duty in Hastings-court, and met the prisoner running from High-street, Shadwell - he stepped aside, and I saw a watch-case in his hand; I seized him - he tussled with me for about two yards, and then dropped the watch - I got him down, and called out, "There is the watch;" I could not get to it myself, and thought some honest person would take it up - I sprang my rattle; a Policeman came and secured him, but the watch was gone; several persons had come up the court.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you say to the prosecutor,"There is your watch, pick it up?" A. It was not there.
The Prisoner put in a written Defence, representing that he was merely running home - declaring his innocence, and stating that Clark was a bad character.
HENRY CLARK. I came up while they were scuffling, but did not take the watch up - I did not want any thing to do with it.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310217-250]
676. JAMES BARKER was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of December, 1 pair of trousers, value 1l.; 2 waistcoats, value 1l.; 1 pair of boots, value 2s.; 1 hat, value 9s., and 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Hugh Edward Turner.
HUGH EDWARD TURNER. I am a brush-maker. On the 28th of December I lodged in Brill-row, in the same room as the prisoner - I got up at eight o'clock in the morning, leaving the prisoner in bed, and the clothes lockedin my box; I returned at night, and on the Friday found my box broken, and the things gone.
MARY HILL. On the 28th the prisoner came to me, and asked if I had a lodging - he slept there one night, and went away, leaving an old hat there.
GEORGE TAYLOR. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner, on the 10th of January, charged with stealing a pair of shoes from Exmouth-street, but have since found these things in pawn.
MR. CLARK. I am a pawnbroker. I have a pair of trousers and a waistcoat, pawned in the name of Johnson, Coleman-street - I do not know who by.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-251]
677. JAMES BRADFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of February, 1 copper, value 12s., the goods of William Read.
WILLIAM READ. I am a wheelwright, and live in Princes-street, Lisson-grove, I lost this copper from a shed at the wharf - I cannot exactly say when; my apprentice first missed it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did he not say he missed it on the 8th? A. No - he mentioned no day.
RICHARD STRATTEN. I am apprentice to the prosecutor. The copper was safe at the wharf on the 31st of January - I missed it on the 3rd of February, and found it at Nash's on the 14th.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you ever say you missed it on the 8th? A. No.
JOSEPH NASH. I am a broker, and dealer in rags and copper. I bought this copper of Mr. Boxall, for 12s. 6d. - Webster came and had it from me: when Read came he had a doubt of it, and I took him to Boxall.
Cross-examined. Q. He had his doubts? A. Yes - he said he could not swear to it himself, but would find a man who could; he said there were three letters on it -Webster cleaned it, but could not find them.
WILLIAM BOXALL. I sold this copper to Nash for 12s. 6d. - I am a rag-merchant, and sometimes deal in stores; my landlord Arbour brought it to me to sell for him.
THOMAS ARBOUR. I am a carpenter. I bought this copper from the prisoner, who was a tenant of mine, on the 5th of February - I set 9s. off his rent for it; he said it was his own - I did not ask where it came from.
Cross-examined. Q. Was any body present when he sold it to you? A. Yes, his wife - he told me he had it from a man who owed him money.
WILLIAM READ. I cannot swear to this copper.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310217-252]
OLD COURT. THURSDAY, FEBRUARY 24.
Fourth Middesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
678. ELIZABETH CLARKE was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Thomas Wilkins, on the 19th of February, and stealing 1 gown, value 7s.; 1 frock, value 1s.; 1 quilt, value 1s.; 1 apron, value 4d.; 1 shift, value 3d., and 1 handkerchief, value 1s., his property.
NATHANIEL WILKINS. I am the son of Thomas Wilkins, who lives in Long's-buildings, Whitecross-street, and is a watchmaker. The prisoner was a stranger there; these things were in a drawer on the first floor - the street. door is kept open for the lodgers; the first floor room was locked, and the key left on the stairs - my mother locked it, as she says, but I do not know that; I stopped the prisoner at the bottom of the stairs, and she dropped the frock on the floor - I did not see it fall; it was found near to where she stood - the other things w0ere found in her apron: my mother claimed them - the prisoner wanted to be let go, to fetch the person who she said had put them into her lap - we did not let her go.
SARAH WILKINS. I am the witness' mother. This gown and frock were on a press bedstead, by the side of the drawers - the other things were in the drawers; I always lock the door when I go out - I had gone out that morning about a quarter-past ten o'clock; I have no servant - my husband was in the workshop on the second floor; I am sure I locked the door, and left the key on the stairs, where I always put it - it was within reach of any body; I returned at a quarter-past eleven, and found the prisoner in custody - the things had been taken from her.
WILLIAM HOOPER. I am a constable. I was sent for and took charge of the prisoner - the property laid on the table, and was claimed by Mr. Wilkins, in the prisoner's hearing; the first words the prisoner said to me were "It was not me opened the door - why don't you go after the person who opened it;" I said, "We will search you first, and then go after her;" I only found 1d. on her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY of stealing only. Aged 60.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18310217-253]
679. CHARLES GAST was indicted for feloniously receiving, on the 5th of January, of a certain evil-disposed person, 1 writing-desk, value 3l., the goods of Peter Wood, well knowing it to have been stolen.
PETER WOOD. I am a writing-desk manufacturer, and live in Portugal-street, Lincoln's Inn-fields. On the 5th of January I went out at nine o'clock, and left these dressing-cases safe in my shop window, close to the door; I returned about one, and they were gone - I saw them at Marlborough-street office about a month after.
WILLIAM BALLARD. On the 5th of February I went with a search-warrant to the prisoner's house in Hanway-yard, and saw this dressing-case standing in the corner of the shop parlour; I went to the house again in about half an hour, and told him in consequence of what I had seen I should take the work-box, as I called it - I think I called it a writing-desk; I went to where I had seen it, and it was gone - I asked where it was; his wife said, "It is here" - I found it moved to the other end of the parlour, on the table: he said it was not his, and that he did not know how it came there - his wife said the same.
NOT GUILTY.

682. ANN TAYLOR was indicted for feloniously receiving, on the 16th of January, 1 medicine-chest, value 2l., the goods of William Martyn, well knowing it to have been stolen.
WILLIAM MARTYN. I live in the Quadrant, Regent-street, and am a chemist and druggist. On Sunday, the 16th of January, about nine o'clock in the morning, I missed a medicine-chest from a glass-case opposite my shop door; I think I had seen it the day before - I found it at the office; I know nothing of the prisoner.
WILLIAM BALLARD. I apprehended the prisoner on the 5th of February at Gast's house; I saw her in the shop - I told her I wished to go into her room; she took me up to a second floor room - this medicine-chest stood on the drawers; I put my hand on it, and said, "Is this yours?" she said, "It is mine," and that she had had it a long time; I said, "Where did you get it?" she made some humming and hesitation, which was not an answer, and I took her in charge.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310217-257]
683. HARRIET DAVIS was indicted for feloniously receiving of a certain evil-disposed person, 1 writing-case, value 16s., and 2 machines for the blind, value 2l., the goods of Ralph Wedgwood, well knowing them to have been stolen.
RALPH WEDGWOOD. I live in Rathbone-place. I lost this writing-case, with a number of others, the latter end of November or beginning of December - I afterwards found it at the office - it is my own manufacture; I am quite sure of it; I lost three machines for the blind at the same time - I have seen them all since; I think I must have seen them in the course of the day they were missed.
THOMAS VESPER. I am a pawnbroker. On the 14th of December the prisoner pawned a machine for the blind for 10s. in the name of Eliza Johnson, No.2, Jeffries-buildings, Westminster; I do not recollect what account she gave - I am certain of her person.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Is it uncommon for people to pawn in a wrong name? A. Not at all.
THOMAS SEAWARD. I am a journeyman to Mr. Harrison, a pawnbroker, of Tottenham-court-road. I produce a portfolio which I took in pawn from the prisoner, in the name of Mrs. Johnson; I have not taken her Christian name - I knew her so well; she said it had been bought at Dover, and cost 24s.
Cross-examined. Q. You knew her well? A. Yes, by frequenting my shop for some time - she gave her right address, No. 7, John-street; I will not swear she said it was bought at Dover, or that she came from Dover.
JOHN KEMP. I am in the employ of Mr. Barret, a pawnbroker of Norfolk-street, Middlesex-hospital. I have a machine for the blind which I took in pawn from the prisoner, for 10s., on the 15th of December, in the name of Johnson, of John-street, who I had repeatedly seen before; I asked how she came by it - she said it was her brother's; I knew she lived in John-street, Tottenham-court-road.
Cross-examined. Q. Is it not common for persons to give erroneous accounts to conceal the fact of their raising money? A. Yes; I had seen her frequently at out shop.
WILLIAM BALLARD. On the 5th of February I was standing between two doors in Gast's house, when the prisoner came in; she put her shoulder against the partition, and called Harry! Harry! to a boy who was in the shop - he saw her, but would not speak to her; she turned round, and saw me standing at a short distance behind; I said, "What is it you want?" she said she only wanted to speak to Harry - I said, "What do you want with him?" she said she had come to fetch a shirt to wash for him, and that she washed for him generally - I took her into the next room, and asked where she lived; she said in Grosvenor-street - I asked which, and after a little hesitation, she said Lower Grosvenor-street; I said "It is impossible that a washerwoman can live in Lower Grosvenor-street, what is the number?" Ellis was in custody in the shop, and desired her to answer no impertinent questions, upon which Ellis was taken away to the office - I said I should not let her go till she satisfied me where she lived - she afterwards said she lived in Colvell-court, which leads out of Charlotte-street; I sent Goddard there with her - in the trunk, at Mr. Thompson's, No. 11, Tottenham-court-road, I found these three duplicates; I went to the pawnbroker's - they relate to these articles.
Cross-examined. Q. Did she know you? A. I am satisfied that she did; but the darkness of the place, and the dirty state she was then in, I did not then know her: Ellis was in custody; I cannot say that he lived in that house; for the room pointed out as his, had neither bed nor bedding in it, nor did he live at Thompson's.
HENRY GODDARD. I am an officer. I received the prisoner from Ballard, and went with her to Colvell-court, Charlotte-street, to a shop there - I asked the landlady, in her hearing, if she lived there; she said Yes, in the first floor back room - I left her there, and from information I afterwards received, I went again, found her in the act of moving away, and took her in charge; the landlord said at the office that Ellis lodged in Colvell-court with her - I do not know whether she heard it.
Cross-examined. Q. She took you to this place as her residence? A. Ballard sent me there with her.
MR. WEDGWOOD. This machine for the blind I have no hesitation in swearing to; here is another, and this is a manifold-writer, not fitted up - I am positive they are all three mine.
Cross-examined. Q. These are part of a number of articles stolen one night in November? A. Or the beginning of December - I sell a great many; these three happen to be the first that I stamped with a tool which I have - I never sold any with the mark of this tool, and these are not fitted up - the prisoner would not know but it was a perfect article.
Prisoner. I leave my defence to my Counsel.
ANN WINCH. I live at No. 37, Compton-street, New-street, and am married - the prisoner is my sister. I do not know that she lived with Ellis - I have heard her say that she washed for him, and she washed a shirt of his in my room, in my presence; I know she has often pawned clothes for him, and redeemed them again.
WILLIAM BALLARD. The room the duplicates were found in contained a quantity of stolen property, but nobody slept there.
CHARLES GEORGE THOMPSON. I live in Tottenham-court-road. I gave Ellis, (who has been convicted.) the use of a room in my house - the officer found the duplicates in that room; I married Ellis' sister - he was a muffin-maker; I knew he was in the habit of attending sales, and purchasing goods; and it was for the purpose of depositing the goods, which I understood he bought, that I let him have the room - I do not know where he slept: I believe he did not sleep at Gast's - I have heard he slept at Colvell-court - if I had wanted to find him in the night, I should have gone to Colvell-court.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Six Months.

NEW COURT. THURSDAY, FEBRUARY 24.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
685. SARAH WILKS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 1 dead fowl, value 3s., the goods of John Edwards.
JOHN EDWARDS. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Crawford-street, Marylebone. On the afternoon of the 19th of February, between three and four o'clock, I was in my shop - I saw the prisoner take the fowl, and place it under her shawl; she then asked Mrs. Edwards for half a quartern of butter - as soon as I could get by two other people I seized her, opened her shawl, and saw the fowl - she said she hoped I would excuse her this time; I am afraid she was under the effects of liquor.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that she had mercly taken up the fowl to enquire the price.
JOHN EDWARDS. The fowl was concealed under her shawl.
GUILTY. Aged 48. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-260]
686. JOHN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 1 bed, value 25s., and 1 sheet, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Rilen.
THOMAS HARRIS. I am a Police-constable. On the morning of the 21st of February I was in the carcase of a house in Marylebone, about a quarter-past eleven o'clock; I saw the prisoner coming from the garden of a cottage near that house with this bed - he fell over some bricks; I said, "You are down;" he said, "I am;" he got up, and went over some vaults with the bed - I followed him across the fields, into Boston-street; I then went up to him, and saw his hands were cut - he said he had cut them in falling down; I asked what he had here; he said, a bed he was going to have emptied for the old woman - I said it was very odd he should come out of the house the back way; I took him to the station - I went to the cottage and found the door open, and the lock of the door was shot - the bedstead was down, and the clothes laid in a heap, just inside the door.
THOMAS RILEN. I have the care of the cottage. I went out at nine o'clock, leaving every thing secure - the back door was fastened with two bolts; I left a chopper in the fire-place - I went out at the front door, and locked it - I came home at twelve, found my back door open, the things strewed about, and the bed and one sheet gone; there was a square of glass out of the kitchen window, and there was the appearance of blood - he had let himself in at the window, and out at the door.
Prisoner's Defence. A man came up to me, and said he would give me 2s. 6d. to carry the bed to Well-street, Oxford-street - I went into the cottage the front way, as far as the back door; the man said, "You may go this way, and I will come after you."
THOMAS HARRIS. He was close to the cottage when I first saw him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-261]
687. GEORGE WATSON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of February, 1 shilling, 1 sixpence, and 8 halfpence, the monies of Robert Smith, from the person of Jane, his wife .
JANE SMITH. I am the wife of Robert Smith. On the 21st of February I was with Jane Harris, in Richmond-street, Westminster - there were a number of persons collected, and a black man was playing some tricks with balls; we stopped to look at him, and I saw the prisoner on my right-hand side - I caught his hand in my right pocket; he drew his hand from me - there were two young fellows behind him, and they took what he had in his hand; I kept him till an officer took him - I missed a shilling, a sixpence, and eight halfpence, which had been safe the moment before.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Have you always told the same story about this? A. Yes - I never let the prisoner go; I seized his hand - he drew his hand back, but I still held his arm; I am quite sure I caught his hand in my pocket - I had taken out a halfpenny for the balck man, and before I had time to give it, the prisoner put his hand into my pocket; I was not at all in liquor - it was not the prisoner who proposed to send for an officer; it was not more than twenty minutes before the officer came - he tried to get away, and said if I were not a woman he would strike me, but the mob said, "You shall not strike her."
JANE HARRIS. I was with Mrs. Smith - I saw her seize the prisoner; I went for the officer.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you been with her long? A. Yes, all the morning - she had nothing to drink in my presence; I had been with her from ten o'clock, and this was about ten minutes past one - I saw her take hold of the prisoner's hand, and draw it out of her pocket-hole; he put his hand behind him, and gave something to a dark young man, who made his way through the mob.
GEORGE BUTLER. I am a Police-constable. I received the prisoner in charge.
Cross-examined. Q. Was the prosecutrix tipsy? A. No; I have heard what she has said - I think she told the samestory at the Police-office; she said she found his hand in her pocket.
Prisoner. Q. Was I not standing with my hands in my pockets, when you came up? A. The prosecutrix had held of your arm.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-262]
688. JOHN REYNOLDS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 umbrella, value 3s., the goods of Peter Gentle.
PETER GENTLE. I live in Pitfield-street, Hoxton, and am an umbrella-maker. On the 16th of February I was at the back of my shop - I heard a string break, and an iron bar jar; I went to the door, and missed an umbrella - I looked out, and saw the prisoner passing the White Horse, next door; he crossed the road, and I saw the umbrella before him - I ran after him, and as he was turning into Charles-square I asked what he was going to do with my umbrella - he made no answer; the umbrella had been within my door - a person could reach it without coming into the shop; it was fastened by a string, which was tied under the ferrule.
JURY. Q. Was there a ticket on it? A. Yes, it is on it now.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was passing - I saw it lay five yards from the door, and took it up.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined Three Months.

690. SARAH LAWSON was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 1 bed, value 30s.; 2 pillows, value 5s.; 2 blankets, value 2s.; 1 couterpane, value 1s., and 1 quilt, value 1s., the goods of Elizabeth Fitzgerald, widow.
ELIZABETH FITZGERALD. I am a widow, and live in Upper Berkeley-street, Portman-square - I have known the prisoner two years; I took her in out of charity four months ago, and continued to support her. On the 19th of February I missed these articles when I came home - the prisoner had left me without notice; I did not see her till the Monday morning following, at ten o'clock.
JAMES CONNOLLY. I am a Police-constable. I looked after the prisoner, and found her at No. 8, Mitcham-street, Paddington - I found this pillow, and several other articles in her room.(Property produced and sworn to)
Prisoner. She has sworn very false - she had not a bed, and her sister gave her 10s. to buy a mattress.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-265]
691. JAMES JACKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of February, 4 sheets, value 6s., and 3 bags, value 6d., the goods of Joseph Powell.
SARAH POWELL. I am the wife of Joseph Powell - he lives in Mansell-street, Goodman's-fields; I was in a cart going along Brick-lane, on the 15th of February, at a quarter before eight o'clock - there was a bag of linen in the cart, which I was bringing from the country to my own house; it contained four sheets and three bags - there was a lad in the cart, but he is not here; a Policeman gave me notice that the property was taken.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Did you see it when you were in Brick-lane? A. No; it was an open cart - it could not have fallen out - I have not promised my witness any thing for her trouble.
CHRISTIANA THOMPSON. I was in Brick-lane, and saw Mrs. Powell and a lad in the cart; I saw a man, whom I think was the prisoner, following and watching the cart - he went behind the cart, and took out this bundle; he was running away - I had before seen a Police-constable, and given information; he took the prisoner with the bundle.
Cross-examined. Q. Did he run? A. Yes, when we cried Stop thief! he ran, but not so much as two or three hundred yards; I had not had any thing to drink with the prosecutrix.
GEORGE BRIDLE. I am an officer. This young woman pointed out to me a person following the cart; I saw him running from it with the bundle - I ran to take him; he threw down the bundle, and I collared him - I never lost sight of him; it was a quarter before eight o'clock.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you quite sure you saw him with the bundle near the cart? A. Yes; I caught him running - there was no crowd there; it was not dark.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not near the cart; I crossed to speak to a person, and they ran and took me.
GUILTY. - Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-266]
692. GEORGE HARRIS, alias GEORGE PEET , JAMES WATTS, RICHARD BAKER, & GEORGE CRONEY were indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 1 wooden till, value 1s.; 1 half-crown, and 2s. 6d. in copper, the property of Henry Thumwood.
HARRIET ASHKETEL. On the 21st of February I saw the four prisoners together in Crane-place, Kentish-town, about three o'clock in the afternoon - I am sure I saw them all; they threw a till over a wall, which attracted my notice - I then saw them passing copper and silver among them, which I saw Harris take out of his hat - he gave them all some; I thought it was not right, and told the Policeman - I took the till up myself; I did not know them before, but I can swear to them all.
WILLIAM MORRIS. I am a Police-constable. This witness gave me the till, and gave me notice of this - I saw all the boys playing with halfpence, opposite her house; I took them - I found 8 1/2d. on Watts, and the rest of the money on the others - here is 1s. 6d. in silver, and this copper - I took two first, and the other two after.
HENRY THUMWOOD. I live in Junction-road, Kentish-town, about a hundred yards from Ashketel's - this is my till; I missed it last Monday afternoon, about two o'clock; it had been drawn out from behind the counter- there was about 4s. or 5s. in it, in halfpence, farthings, sixpences, and shillings; I did not see the prisoners near there.
Harris' Defence. Baker asked me to go with him to his father - we went, and he asked these two boys the nearest way to Highgate, and then the Policeman took them.
Baker's Defence. We asked these two boys the way; the Policeman took them, then called us, and took us.
Watts' Defence. I was going after a place, and was talking to these boys.
HARRIS - GUILTY. Aged 13.
WATTS - GUILTY. Aged 14.
BAKER - GUILTY. Aged 13.
CRONEY - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-267]
693. LETITIA IZZARD was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 7 caps, value 2s.; 1 shirt, value 6d.; 1 frock, value 4d.; 1 handkerchief, value 1d., and 1 towel, value 1d., the goods of Joseph Hurst.
MARTHA HURST. I am the wife of Joseph Hurst - we live in Belton-street, Long-acre. I went out on an errand about four o'clock in the afternoon last Saturday; I had left the property stated hanging to dry on the landing, at the top of the house, three stories high - the street door is always open - it is a broker's shop; I returned in about five minutes, and saw the prisoner coming down my own staircase on the the third floor - I asked who she had been to? she made no answer - I looked about, and missed the property; I called after her, and overtook her on the first floor landing - I seized a basket from her hand, with part of my property in it; a witness took two caps from her bosom, and five from her pocket; they were all wet.
RACHAEL PEARSON. I lodge in the second floor room. I was cleaning the landing, and saw the prisoner with a basket - I took seven caps from her bosom and her pocket - I kept her till the Policeman came.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I unfortunately went up stairs, and the things had fallen, but I did not put them into my basket; I took them up, but did not mean to take them away.
GUILTY. Aged 32. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-268]
694. JAMES HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 cap, value 3s., the goods of Sarah Green, from the person of Frederick Gilbert Green .
ELIZA COLE. I was in South-street, St. Luke's, on Thursday last, between three and four o'clock in the afternoon - I saw a little boy with a cap on; the prisoner took it off his head, and ran away - the lamplighter stopped him in my presence, took the cap from him, and gave it to me.
WILLIAM CROCKLEY. I am a lamplighter. I saw the prisoner running with the cap under his jacket, as I was cleaning a lamp at the corner of South-street - I got down the ladder, and took him in Ropemaker-street; I took the cap, and gave it to Cole.
SARAH GREEN. I am the mother of Frederick Gilbert Green - I am a widow. This is my cap, and my son had it on; the prisoner was brought to my shop, and I gave charge of him.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutrix.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310217-269]
695. ANN GOWER was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 1 handkerchief, value 2d.; 1 purse, value 2d., and 1 sovereign, the property of Jeremiah McGurrin, from his person.
JEREMIAH McGURRIN. I keep a beer and porter shop in Barbican. On the 4th of February I received a sovereign and 2s. 6d. in silver, at Shadwell, in the evening - I then went with two other persons, and had 1 1/2 lb. of steaks, two pots of porter, and three four-penny worths of gin-and-water between us; I then went to another public-house, where I saw the prisoner - she asked me to treat her, which I did; we were there two or three hours - we had about two quarterns of gin, and I had four pennyworth of gin-and-water; I was not drunk - we came together to Red Lion-market, Whitecross-street; she followed me nearly to my own house - I had not known her before; I had taken out my bag and paid for the liquor at the public-house - I felt in it a sovereign, and 1s. 6d., and I had 6d. worth of copper in the same pocket - when I got near to Red Lion-market I had occasion to stop; I took my bag from my right-hand breeches pocket, and thrust it into my bosom for safety, as I thought the prisoner followed me too long; no sooner had I done that than she snatched the bag from my bosom, and ran away - I sung out Stop thief! she ran out of the market; I made a rush to catch her, and fell on my knees - she ran out, but came into the market again, and the Policeman took her; he found on her my pocket handkerchief, but none of the money, nor the bag - I was sober, and I swear she took it from me; I did not like her being so long about me, and wanted to get from her; I am married.
GEORGE GLADWELL. I am a Police-constable. I heard the alarm, and saw the prisoner run about twenty yards before I caught her; the prosecutor said she had robbed him of a sovereign, and a bag - the flap of his breeches was undone, and the knees muddy: I found the handkerchief on the prisoner, a sixpence, and 4d. in copper; the prosecutor was the worse for liquor, but not drunk.
JEREMIAH McGURRIN. This handkerchief was in my coat pocket - I did not give it to her.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that the prosecutor, who was intoxicated, had followed her, endeavouring to prevail on her to accompany him, but that she had not robbed him.
GUILTY of stealing the handkerchief. Aged 24.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-270]
696. JAMES CLARK was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of December, 1 coat, value 2l. 10s., and 1 hat, value 10s., the goods of Thomas Ellis.
THOMAS ELLIS. I am a sawyer, and live in Crown-street, Soho - the prisoner lodged in the same house, and slept with me; he was errand-boy to Mr. Matthews. On the 28th of December I got up first in the morning, and left my coat and hat in the room - I returned at five o'clockin the evening; they were gone, and so was the prisoner -I found him last Wednesday week; I asked him about my coat and hat, and he said he had sold them to a Jew for 10s.
JANE EDWARDS. I live in the house . The prisoner told me that Thomas Ellis had sent him for his coat and hat - that he was at his uncle's, and was tipsy ; he went up stairs , got them , and took them away.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310217-271]
697. WILLIAM SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of February, 1 pair of trousers , value 8s., and 1 waistcoat , value 4s., the goods of Frederick Best.
FREDERICK BEST. I am groom to Mr. Isaacs , and lodge in Cox-court - the prisoner lodged there, and slept with me. I got up on the 1st of February , and left my trousers , waistcoat , and the prisoner in the room; I returned at night , but did not miss them till next morning - the prisoner slept there that second night; I spoke to him about them - he said he could answer for himself that he had not had them .
CHARLES WORLEY. I am a pawnbroker . I have a pair of trousers and a waistcoat , pawned by the prisoner, on the 1st of February .
Prisoner. I have often pawned them for him .
FREDERICK BEST. I have asked him to pawn them for myself , but not at that time ; he has pawned them , and given me the money .
JURY. Q. Has he ever pawned them for his own accomodation ? A. No, only for me - I have lent him 1s. or 2s. out of it; he had no authority to pawn them .
NOT GUILTY.
